#### Battery replay and currency overlay (end-of-run consistency pass)

**Replay.** Every logged battery from G1 to G410 was rerun against the final corpus (`tools/replay2.py`, results in `work/scratch/replay.jsonl`, summary by `tools/replaysum.py`). G411 was run by checker 2B on the final corpus after the replay began, so it needed no replay. Result: 410 batteries, 1,264 terms. 1,203 terms returned exactly the logged hits; 61 returned the logged hits plus more (superset growth, every case caused by federal or rule texts fetched after the battery ran); 0 terms lost a logged hit or fell in count. The controls passed on every rerun.

Three terms went from 0 hits to 1, the only growth that could touch a confirmed absence. Each new hit was opened:
- G72 moveout-clean: Okla. Admin. Code § 310:285-1-2, the lodging-establishment definitions ("clean" defined for hotel rooms, "vacationing public" in the cabin definition). Not a residential move-out cleaning rule; `edu-no-carpet-cleaning-rule-ok` stands.
- G78 subsidy-late: FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.451, the HAP contract rule on penalties against the housing authority for late housing assistance payments. The main pass fetched this section for `edu-no-subsidy-late-fee-rule-ok`, and that row already states its conditions; the row's Oklahoma absence finding is unchanged.
- G164 owner-liable: Okla. Admin. Code § 35:30-29-39, closure of a bulk fertilizer storage facility. Not a utility rule; `edu-no-municipal-utility-lien-ok` stands.

No row changed as a result of the replay.

**Currency overlay.** The rows, tags, proposed rows and this log cite 3,321 Oklahoma statute sections. 238 of them carry a 2026 history line in the corpus (statutes updated through November 12, 2026); each was checked against the row text that cites it. Texts that take effect after today are stated with their dates wherever a row relies on them: SB 893 (2026), Okla. Stat. tit. 60, §§ 128.1-128.4, effective July 1, 2027, in `edu-military-air-zone-disclosure-ok`; the roofing contractor exemption in Okla. Stat. tit. 59, § 1151.9(B)(9) (2026 Okla. Sess. Laws ch. 317, effective January 1, 2027) in `edu-landlord-registration-ok` and `edu-portfolio-thresholds-ok`. The other 115 citations of future-effective sections (among them the 2026 Okla. Sess. Laws ch. 445 sections effective January 1, 2027) appear only inside battery hit lists, where they are named as searched units and no row states their text as current law.

### §4 Decisions and questions asked of Taylor

**Class decisions** (the full text, with the statute quoted, was given to every helper as `class-decisions.md`; rows cite them as "gaps log §4, class decision N"):
1. CD1 prohibited lease terms (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)-(B); unenforceable, no stated penalty).
2. CD2 conflicting terms unenforceable (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 103(B)).
3. CD3 arrangements outside the Act (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 104).
4. CD4 recurring definitions: rent, deposit, rental agreement, single-family residence (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 102).
5. CD5 service of termination notices (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111(E)).
6. CD6 tenant repairs and chores only by a conspicuous separate writing (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(B)); Taylor's 2026-09-30 decision stands.
7. CD7 good faith, mitigation, prevailing-party fees, settlement, liberal construction (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, §§ 105-107, 135).
8. CD8 fixed charges and the penalty doctrine (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, §§ 213-215; Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 97); case law not read, cautious course stated, rows stay VERIFIED.
9. CD9 the 2026-09-30 declines stand and get education rows (rule 54).
10. CD10 the NEEDS_REVIEW policy (SOP Part 5): only an unreachable primary source keeps a row open.
11. CD11 clauses that only restate law are education rows (rule 55; Taylor 2026-10-24).
12. CD12 local ordinances named, not researched (rule 3).
13. CD13 2026 acts with dated effect: read each act's effective-date section (rule 16).

**Variables.** No new `{{variables}}`: the delta and the proposed rows use 9 variables, all already in the library.

**Main-pass and helper decisions:**

- (MAIN) Item 8, smoking-policy (central edit of 2026-10-04 naming whose smoking Tenant pays for): CONFIRMED for OK. The edit only narrows Tenant's liability to smoking by Tenant, an occupant, or a guest or invitee, which matches the tenant's own statutory duty not to 'permit any person, animal or pet' to damage the premises (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(5)); it is not an exculpation or indemnity of liability caused by the landlord's acts that Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(4) bars. A property owner may still prohibit 'the consumption of medical marijuana or medical marijuana product by smoke or vaporization on the premises, within the structures of the premises or within ten (10) feet of the entryway' (Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 427.8(C)); HB 3127 (2026) amended Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 427.8 effective November 1, 2026 ("Laws 2026, c. 20, § 1, eff. Nov. 1, 2026", history line) and (C) still reads so. Smoked or vaped medical marijuana is 'subject to the same restrictions for tobacco' under the Smoking in Public Places and Indoor Workplaces Act (Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 427.8(L)); that Act limits smoking in public places and indoor workplaces, excludes a private residence from its "public place" definition except areas used as a licensed child care facility (Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1-1523(D)), and nothing in it restricts a landlord's smoking ban. Read section-open 2026-11-13 from the OK corpus: Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 427.8, Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1-1521, Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1-1522, Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1-1523, Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1-1525, Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 1247, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127.
- (MAIN) Item 8, addendum-precedence (central edit of 2026-10-04 letting a document the law says controls over the lease do so): CONFIRMED for OK; the edit only defers to law. The notices Oklahoma requires still control: the owner/manager and service disclosure must be made 'As a part of any rental agreement' and 'prominently and in writing' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 116(A); landlord-disclosure-ok); known flooding within five years must be included 'prominently and in writing as part of any written rental agreements' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113a(A); flood-disclosure-ok); the methamphetamine disclosure (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(C); meth-disclosure-ok) and the federal lead disclosure (lead-based-paint) fall within the clause's "disclosure, notice, or addendum that applicable law requires". The separate tenant maintenance agreement is a 'conspicuous writing independent of the rental agreement' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(B)), not an attached addendum, and tenant-repair-agreement-ok says it is separate from the Lease, so the clause does not subordinate it. Read section-open 2026-11-13: Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113a, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 116, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118.
- (MAIN) Item 8, hoa-compliance (fine pass-through): CONFIRMED for OK, no conflict. A lease may not provide for exculpation, limitation or indemnification of liability 'for damages or injuries to persons or property caused by or resulting from the acts or omissions of either party' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(4)); an association fine levied for the tenant's own rule violation is not such damage or injury, and the clause charges Tenant only for fines "due to Tenant's violation". A fine payable to the landlord under the lease is 'rent' by definition (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 102(11)). The Unit Ownership Estate Act binds tenants directly: 'All unit owners, tenants of such owners, employees of owners and tenants ... shall be subject to this act and to the declaration and bylaws' (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 530); unit owners must comply strictly with bylaws and rules (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 508). The real estate development act lets an owners association enforce obligations by assessment that 'may become a lien' on defaulting owners' lots (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 852(C)); it puts no limit on passing a fine to a tenant. Read section-open 2026-11-13: Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 508, Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 524, Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 530, Okla. Stat. tit. 60, §§ 851-858, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113. Topics hoa, association-obligations-disclosure and political-access are answered in slices S11 and S04.
- (MAIN) Item 9(d), CARES: CONFIRMED, no OK eviction row contradicts edu-cares-act-notice (tagged OK), which says the federal 30-day notice to vacate applies to covered properties "on top of your state's notices" (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c), read 2026-11-13 from uscode.house.gov). edu-nonpayment-notice-ok, edu-noncompliance-notice-ok, edu-eviction-process-ok, edu-for-cause-eviction-ok, periodic-tenancy-notice-ok and criminal-activity-ok each state Oklahoma's own notice or cure rule (criminal-activity-ok says "Under Oklahoma law"); none says it is the only notice required or denies a federal notice. Optional product note (not a defect): edu-nonpayment-notice-ok and edu-noncompliance-notice-ok could add one pointer sentence to edu-cares-act-notice for covered properties; the missing topics rent-demand-bar, expedited-criminal-eviction and landlord-remedies-termination are answered in slice S08.
- (MAIN) Item 10, binding-arbitration: NOT OFFERED for Oklahoma; the shared arbitration-agreement clause is not tagged. Oklahoma's Uniform Arbitration Act validates a recorded agreement to arbitrate future disputes (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1857(A)) and excludes no lease (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1855(D)), and no statute penalizes including or suing to enforce a prohibited lease term (G32; Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(B), unenforceability only). But whether moving Landlord and Tenant Act claims to arbitration is a waiver of the tenant's remedies, which a lease may not require (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1)), when those remedies are enforceable 'in any court of appropriate jurisdiction including small claims court' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 105(B)), turns only on case law (none read). Under Taylor's rule (2026-11-07) the library does not offer the clause; edu-binding-arbitration-ok says so. S01's proposed tag note is recorded in its decision object and was not applied.
- (MAIN) Fetch resolution, International Residential Code section R315 and International Fire Code section 915 (S01 request): not fetched. Oklahoma adopts the IRC 2024 by reference (Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-6-1) and amends Chapter 3 without touching the alarm sections (Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-6-8); the ICC base text is copyrighted model code that the Administrative Code does not publish, so no official Oklahoma route prints it. edu-confirmed-absences-habitability-ok item (7) states the adoption and the bound.
- (MAIN) Fetch resolutions after batch 1 (S01-S03): Okla. Const. art. II, § 19 and Okla. Const. art. XIV, §§ 2-3 are current despite the 2022 constitution file: the Secretary of State's State Questions index (SQ 814 to SQ 848, read 2026-11-13 in the built-in browser) lists no state question since that file touching art. II or art. XIV; the one constitutional amendment adopted, SQ 834 (2024), amends Okla. Const. art. III, § 1 (edu-jury-waiver-ok, edu-statutory-caps-ok revised). FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 1681s-2, FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 1692a, FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 1692f, FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.451 and FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.305 read 2026-11-13 and saved; edu-no-rent-reporting-rule-ok, edu-collection-fee-ok and edu-no-subsidy-late-fee-rule-ok revised to state them. The HUD tenancy addendum form (HUD-52641-A) was not read; the row states its controlling effect from FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 982.308(f)(2). OSCN (Rules for District Courts): Taylor tried the Turnstile check on 2026-11-13 and it failed; rows needing a District Court rule stay NEEDS_REVIEW naming the rule set (Part 5: a primary source that can't be reached).
- (MAIN) Surety bond in disability-modification-restoration-ok (S04 asked Taylor; decided by the main pass as a drafting judgment, rule 76): the bond stays, offered only as the tenant's alternative to the federal interest-bearing escrow and only where restoration is needed for tenants without disabilities and security is necessary. Federal law invalidates a state law only to the extent it 'purports to require or permit any action that would be a discriminatory housing practice under this subchapter' (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 3615); because the tenant may always choose the escrow that FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(a) allows, the clause never insists on a bond, whatever a court would say of a bond demand. Rows disability-modification-restoration-ok and edu-disability-accommodation-ok now cite FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 3615 (errata M-E5 to M-E7). No question to Taylor.
- (MAIN) edu-no-protected-class-inquiry-ban-ok: FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.202 and FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.75 were read 2026-11-13 in the built-in browser (eCFR); the row now states the federal disability-inquiry ban and its five all-applicant exceptions and that the statement ban covers applications, and is VERIFIED (errata M-E8 to M-E12).
- (MAIN) condemned-premises-rent-bar (S05 asked Taylor; decided by the main pass): the library does not offer a clause ending the lease on a public taking or allocating the condemnation award in Oklahoma. Its terms would turn only on eminent-domain case law that was not read, and the library does not offer an option that rests only on unread case law (the same rule Taylor set for arbitration, kickoff item 10). `edu-condemned-premises-rent-ok` records the option as lawful but not offered (rule 54) and points to the 90-day notice in Okla. Stat. tit. 27, § 13. No question to Taylor.
- (MAIN) Fetch resolutions for S05 (rule 36): every S05 fetch request was read in the built-in browser on 2026-11-13 and the rows revised; the two NEEDS_REVIEW rows (`edu-health-district-rental-rules-ok`, `edu-sanitary-code-variance-ok`) are now VERIFIED because the State Board of Health chapter list has no housing or rental sanitation chapter and the lodging rules 'shall apply only to guest rooms and any supporting facilities' (Okla. Admin. Code § 310:285-1-1.1). Details are in the fetch-resolution list that follows the findings for other states.
- (MAIN) Utility billing clause (S06 asked Taylor; decided by the main pass): the library does not offer an Oklahoma clause for billing tenants for city-bought water or sewer by submeter or ratio formula. It would be lawful only within the cost-plus-10% cap and per-bill disclosure of Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1, could not reach Commission-regulated gas (Okla. Admin. Code § 165:45-7-11(j)), would need a Commission-approved submeter system for electricity (Okla. Admin. Code § 165:35-13-7(e)), and its allocation terms would rest on case law that was not read. `edu-utility-apportionment-ok` records the option as lawful but not offered (rule 54); the single-meter allowance clause `utility-allowance-cap-ok` and the transfer clause `utility-transfer-ok` cover the safe cases. No question to Taylor.
- (MAIN) private-well-testing (S06 fetch): the DEQ rules read 2026-11-13 make a well that serves more than one dwelling or a multi-family dwelling a minor public water supply system, with DEQ authorization, annual laboratory coliform testing and immediate notice to consumers (Okla. Admin. Code § 252:624-1-2; Okla. Admin. Code § 252:624-7-1), and a system with 15 connections or 25 people a public water supply system under the incorporated federal rules (Okla. Admin. Code § 252:631-1-2). The NEEDS_REVIEW row `edu-no-private-well-testing-ok` was withdrawn and replaced by `edu-private-well-testing-ok` (VERIFIED), whose title no longer says there is no rule.
- (MAIN) pest-control-notice (S07 fetch): the Board of Agriculture rules (Okla. Admin. Code ch. 35:30) were saved 2026-11-13 from rules.ok.gov and screened; they set no general tenant-notice duty for pest control and require notice, vacating and signs only for building fumigation (Okla. Admin. Code § 35:30-17-82). `edu-pest-control-notice-ok` was revised and made VERIFIED (errata M-E49 to M-E51). S07 also corrected the brief's fire-code note: the IFC 2024 reaches existing buildings through its adopted Chapter 11 (Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-4-16), not through Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-4-1 itself; known-shapes item 5 was reworded.
- (MAIN) Check round 1 part C notes, decided by the main pass: (1) `furnishings-included-la` is no longer tagged OK; its return promise has no fault limb, the same reason `waterbed-mn` was declined, and `furnishings-included-ok` adds the deliberate-or-negligent limb (consistency with Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(5)). (2) `utility-transfer-ok` keeps its promise not to shut off service while an account stays in the landlord's name: closing service to press a tenant could be argued to be a wrongful exclusion (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 123) and only unread case law would settle it, so the protective wording stays, as an optional clause the landlord may decline. (3) The 10% resale cap is read as a markup above cost, the clauses are safe on either reading, and the reading is stated in `edu-utility-resale-cap-ok`. (4) The energy-data clause's self-limits are marked as not required by Oklahoma law. (5) The fire-code vacancy paraphrase now carries the rule's limiter.
- (MAIN) cold-weather-vacate-notice (S08 asked Taylor; decided by the main pass under Taylor's standing chores rule, class decision CD6): the Oklahoma cold-weather clause goes in the lease. Keeping the heat on at a minimum setting and giving notice of an absence are 'reasonable limitations upon use' a rental agreement may set (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 117(B)) and a reporting duty, not 'specified repairs, maintenance tasks, alterations or remodeling' that need a separate writing (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(B)). `cold-weather-vacate-notice-ok` is offered as an optional clause whose damage sentence never reaches the landlord's own heating or plumbing maintenance failures (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(A)(3); Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(4)). No question to Taylor.

Stat. tit. 27, § 15; errata M-E61, M-E62); the findings on existing rows `edu-no-nuisance-eviction-rule-ok` (gambling-lease voiding, Okla. Stat. tit. 21, §§ 945, 958) and `edu-holdover-ok` (Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 70) stay with the findings on existing rows for sync.
- (MAIN) Association approval (S11 asked Taylor; decided by the main pass): the library offers an optional CONDITIONAL Oklahoma clause, `association-approval-ok`, in place of the shared `association-approval-la`. Its only landlord-side term is the tenant's duty to apply to the association and cooperate; it gives the landlord no right to cancel, and it keeps the tenant's remedies if the landlord cannot deliver possession because approval is refused or delayed (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 120(A)). No question to Taylor.
- (MAIN) NEEDS_REVIEW at the end of the canvass, two rows: `edu-entity-landlord-court-representation-ok` (the Rules for District Courts, the Supreme Court's practice-of-law rules and county local rules are on OSCN, which served a Turnstile challenge Taylor could not clear; the small-claims rule in Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1751(G) is stated as settled) and `edu-communal-land-lease-approval-ok` (for restricted land of members of the Five Civilized Tribes only: the special acts of Congress that part 162 defers to under FEDERAL: 25 C.F.R. § 162.006(b)(3) were not identified in any held source; the rest of the row is read and stated, with the cautious course of getting a BIA-approved lease or written confirmation that none is needed). Both rows state a cautious course.
- (MAIN) Item 8, no-alterations (central edit of 2026-10-04 adding that the clause does not change who owns an installation the law makes Tenant's property): CONFIRMED for OK; the edit only defers to law. Two Oklahoma rules it meets: (1) the disability-modification right, 'a refusal to permit, at the expense of the disabled person, reasonable modifications of existing premises ... provided that such person also provides a surety bond guaranteeing restoration of the premises to their prior condition, if necessary to make the premises suitable for nondisabled tenants' (Okla. Stat. tit. 25, § 1452(A)(16)(a)), preserved by the clause's "reasonable modification that applicable law entitles Tenant to perform"; (2) the general fixture rule, under which 'a tenant may remove from the demised premises at any time during the continuance of his term any thing affixed thereto for purpose of trade, manufacture, ornament or domestic use, if the removal can be effected without injury to the premises' (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 334). Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 334 sits outside the Landlord and Tenant Act and carries no non-waiver words, so the clause's agreement that an approved alteration remains part of the property is a contract term whose effect against that removal right is case law (no Oklahoma case read); the new sentence leaves any right the law gives Tenant in place. Telecom, solar and EV-charging access statutes are answered by slice S11 (telecom-access, portable-solar, ev-charging-*); see gaps log §2. Read section-open 2026-11-13: Okla. Stat. tit. 25, § 1452, Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 334, Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 7.
- (MAIN) Check round 1 part H notes, decided by the main pass: (1) the Five Civilized Tribes bound on `edu-communal-land-lease-approval-ok` stands and the row stays NEEDS_REVIEW (the checker agrees; the special acts are not held); (2) the errata statement in `edu-frozen-standard-incorporation-ok` now names the adopted codes whose rules carry an errata sentence, since the existing building code rule (Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-8-1) has none (M-E109); (3) the third reason S11 gave for declining `ev-charger-installation-hi` (a no-fault repair promise) is withdrawn, because damage from the tenant's own installation or removal is caused by the tenant's side; the decline stands on its other two reasons (above-cost reimbursement against Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 161.1, and a deposit-cap premise Oklahoma lacks); (4) a citation slip in the `no-alterations` finding and a quote cut before its if-limb in the item 8 decision were restated.
- (MAIN) Check round 2 part 2 follow-ups (made without Taylor; cautious courses, rows stay VERIFIED): `edu-rent-into-court-counterclaim-ok` now tells the landlord that any tenant who appeals an eviction judgment must keep paying current rent into court, because the abandonment sentence of Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.10A(F) is not worded only for bonded appeals and no court rule or case was read (M-E120, M-E121). The proposed `landlord-maintenance-ok` no longer subjects the landlord's promise to "Tenant's own maintenance obligations under this Lease"; it refers to Tenant's statutory duties and any separate written repair agreement, so tenant repairs stay in `tenant-repair-agreement-ok` as class decision CD6 requires (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(B)).
- (S01) showing-notice-waiver (OK): shared `showing-notice-la` not tagged for Oklahoma. Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 128(C) fixes at least one day's notice (except emergency or impracticability), and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1) bars a lease waiving rights under the Act; the clause's same-day bracket would waive it and is lawful only on a bracket condition (rule 26). No OK clause offered: one day or more only restates the statute, already covered by `landlords-access`.
- (S01) homestead-waiver (OK): the OK log §6.1 decline stands, but its reason is restated. The 2026-09-30 log cited Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1); the homestead and personal-property exemptions are rights under Okla. Stat. tit. 31, § 1 and Okla. Const. art. XII, not rights under the Landlord and Tenant Act, so (A)(1) does not clearly reach them. The decline rests instead on: no statute authorizing a lease waiver (G38), the lien bar of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(5) for any pledge of exempt property, and the fact that a renter's home is not a homestead (owned land, Okla. Stat. tit. 31, § 2(C)). Recorded in `edu-homestead-waiver-ok`.
- (S01) binding-arbitration (OK), kickoff item 10: PROPOSE tagging shared `arbitration-agreement` OK; not tagged here. (1) Law allows it: Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1857(A) validates a record agreement to arbitrate future controversies; Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1855(D) excludes only collective bargaining and insurance-referencing contracts (no consumer, adhesion, tort or residential exclusion; G48). Carve-outs fit: (b)(1) possession plus joined rent and damage claims stays in the district court (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.1); (b)(2) small claims (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1751, $10,000); (b)(3) deposit claims stay in court (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 115(E)); (c) keeps every remedy and the right to a lawyer (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1855(B)(4), Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1867); (d) county location and landlord-paid fees answer the Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1880(B)(2) review factors; (e) opt-out. Fee screen: (c) lets the arbitrator award fees a court could award (statutory fees under Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 105(B)) and (d) allocates arbitration costs, not attorney fees, so Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(3) is not engaged; nothing exculpates or creates a lien ((A)(4)-(5)). (2) No penalty: no statute penalizes including, or suing to enforce, a prohibited term (G32; Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(B) unenforceability only); the Consumer Protection Act lists no lease term. Residual doubt, stated in `edu-binding-arbitration-ok`: whether arbitrating Act claims is a waiver of remedies barred by Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1) given that Act rights are enforceable 'in any court of appropriate jurisdiction including small claims court' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 105(B)); case law, not read. If the main pass reads the item-10 rule so that this doubt means the answer turns only on case law, decline the tag and replace the last two sentences of `edu-binding-arbitration-ok` with: "Because that question is unsettled, this library does not offer an arbitration clause for Oklahoma; if you want one, have an Oklahoma lawyer draft it, keep it mutual and leave eviction cases, small claims and the tenant's statutory remedies in court." Proposed tag note: "OK: Applies as written. Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1857(A) validates the provision; Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1855(D) excludes no lease; possession stays in the district court (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.1); small claims (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1751); right to a lawyer kept (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1855(B)(4)); standard-form review (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1880(B)); no knowing-use penalty (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(B); G32); the clause allocates no attorney fees of its own, so Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(3) is not engaged. Whether arbitration of Act claims is a waiver barred by Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1) is case law, not read; see edu-binding-arbitration-ok."
- (S02) deposit-cost-schedule (OK): no pre-set deposit charge clause offered. No statute authorizes one; a pre-set amount for breach stands only under Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 215(A), and how Oklahoma courts treat cleaning or turnover charges is unread case law (gaps log §4, class decision 8). Education row states the option, why it is not offered, and the cautious course.
- (S02) fee-in-lieu-of-deposit (OK): a fee in place of a deposit is lawful in Oklahoma as a fee (rent under Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 102) and unregulated, but the library does not offer a clause: its terms depend on the third-party product, and whether such a product is insurance or a surety under Title 36 was not researched. Education row states the option, why it is not offered and the landlord's own-clause route (rule 54).
- (S02) rent-receipt-anti-waiver (OK): the brief's lead (leads-2026-09-30 §18.2, row "rent-receipt-anti-waiver | AE | edu-no-rent-receipt-rule-ok") answers a different subject (receipts for rent). The corpus holds the uniform-act analogue: 'Any agreement, assignment, conveyance, trust deed or security instrument which authorizes a person other than the beneficial owner to act as landlord of a dwelling unit shall not relieve the beneficial owner of the duty to conform with this act' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 108). Answered Present with edu-rent-receipt-anti-waiver-ok.
- (S02) tax-escalation and rent-escalation (OK): rent-escalation-ga (GA, TX; utilities, taxes or insurance pass-through, keyed rent-escalation) is not tagged OK. Taxes and insurance are covered by the tagged tax-insurance-escalation-la, and its utility limb would pass through utility costs as rent without the bill disclosure and 10% resale cap that Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 161.1 imposes on resold city electricity or gas, so as written it omits an Oklahoma limit.
- (S03) Kickoff item 11 (Consumer Credit Code rate limits incorporated by Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 266): late-fee-limit does not turn on them. The Code's delinquency-charge limit applies 'With respect to a consumer credit sale, refinancing, or consolidation, including a revolving charge account' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-203(1)); a consumer credit sale is 'a sale of goods, services or an interest in land' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-104(1)) and a 'Consumer lease' is 'a lease of goods' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-106(1)). A residential lease is neither, so edu-no-late-fee-limit-ok states that the Code's limits do not reach it. Whether the statutory-caps topic turns on the interest-rate limits is decided in that topic.
- (S03) governmental-fines (restates the withdrawn decision at S03.jsonl line 24): tagged tenant-caused-fines-oh (OH, AL) for OK and did NOT tag tenant-caused-fines-as. The -as text has Tenant "reimburse" Landlord, an indemnity shape; a rental agreement may not provide for 'the exculpation, limitation or indemnification of any liability arising under law for damages or injuries to persons or property' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(4)), and how far that reaches is unread case law (OK log §7). The -oh text makes the tenant-caused condition a breach and measures recovery as actual damages, which stays outside the indemnity shape. Cautious course chosen; no question to Taylor needed.
- (S04) disability-accommodation: shared clause disability-modification-restoration-mn NOT tagged OK; it omits the surety-bond mechanism in Okla. Stat. tit. 25, § 1452(A)(16)(a) and the federal escrow limit in FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(a). New optional clause disability-modification-restoration-ok offers the bond only where restoration is needed for nondisabled tenants and necessary to secure funds, with a tenant-chosen interest-bearing escrow alternative.
- (S04) trespass-notice: shared clause barred-person-covenant-ne NOT tagged OK; its victim carve-out copies Nebraska's list and is narrower than Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113.3 (any victim or alleged victim, regardless of a protective order). New optional clause barred-person-covenant-ok uses the same covenant with an Oklahoma carve-out.
- (S05) landlord-registration: Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 22-110.1(B) bars a municipal registration ordinance itself, not only registration fees: 'No municipality shall enact or attempt to enforce through fees, civil fines or criminal penalties any ordinance, rule or regulation to require the registration of real property.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 22-110.1(B)). The fee-only bar is subsection (C)'s, on the contact information a city may collect. The rows say registration is barred and contact information is fee-free; whether a city rental licence is a registration of real property is left as unread case law with the cautious course (get local advice).
- (S05) Kickoff Oklahoma item 6 (preemption reach), per topic: Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 14-101.1(A) reaches only an ordinance 'which regulates the amount of rent to be charged' (Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 14-101.1(A)); Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 22-110.1(B) reaches only municipal ordinances 'to require the registration of real property' (Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 22-110.1(B)), and keeps lists, contact information for nuisance and dilapidated property, abatement contacts and occupancy standards (Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 22-110.1(C)-(E)). Reach: landlord-registration YES (registration barred; contact information fee-free); rental-inspection NO (occupancy standards kept; a registration-linked program is unread case law); lease-registration NO (county-clerk recording is Title 16 law, not municipal registration); good-cause-notice NO; rights-responsibilities-guide NO; single-family-zone-lease-limit NO (zoning, Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 43-101); accessory-dwelling-unit NO; health-district-rental-rules NO (Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1-209); lead-safe-certification NO; substandard-property-receivership NO (subsections (C) and (E) expressly preserve dilapidated-property and abatement contacts); inspection-condemnation-disclosure NO; condemned-premises-rent-bar NO. State unsafe and dilapidated building statutes read: Okla. Stat. tit. 11, §§ 22-112, 22-112.1, 22-112.3, 22-112.4, 22-121, 22-140 to 22-140.5, Okla. Stat. tit. 19, §§ 360, 863.44, 863.45, 863.47, 863.48, Okla. Stat. tit. 68, § 3134.1, Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1080.
- (S06) utility-apportionment (OK): the shared clause utility-billing-wv (WV, AL) is not tagged OK and no OK billing clause is drafted. Gas cannot be separately metered or submetered and billed (Okla. Admin. Code § 165:45-7-11(j)); electric submetering needs Commission approval (Okla. Admin. Code § 165:35-13-7(e)); city-bought electricity, gas, water and sewer resold to residential lessees carry a cost-plus-10% cap and per-unit-cost bill disclosure (Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1; Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 161.1). The option is lawful only on conditions that turn on the supplier, so edu-utility-apportionment-ok records it as lawful in some arrangements and not offered (rule 54).
- (S06) utility-transfer (OK): utility-transfer-nj is not tagged; utility-transfer-ok is offered instead. It drops the shared clause's "not Rent" sentence (rent is every payment to the landlord under the rental agreement except deposits and damages, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 102(11)) and builds in the per-unit-cost and fee disclosure and the no-markup limit that Okla. Stat. tit. 17, §§ 151.1(B) and 161.1(B) require of a reseller of city-bought utilities.
- (S06) utility-allowance-cap (OK): utility-allowance-cap-co is not tagged (its "will not be characterized as Rent" sentence conflicts with Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 102(11), and its bill proof lacks the disclosure of Okla. Stat. tit. 17, §§ 151.1(B) and 161.1(B)). utility-allowance-cap-ok is offered, limited to a utility on a meter serving only the Premises and excluding Commission-regulated natural gas because of Okla. Admin. Code § 165:45-7-11(j).
- (S06) Leads corrected by the corpus (OK log §18.2 of 2026-09-30, leads only): utility-allowance-cap was recorded NA with the reason "No statute", but Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1 (water and sewer) and Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 161.1 (electricity and gas) cap rebilled charges at cost plus 10% with bill disclosure; utility-apportionment cited only Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 161.1, but Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1 and the Commission rules Okla. Admin. Code § 165:45-7-11(j) and Okla. Admin. Code § 165:35-13-7(c) also govern; utility-shutoff-statute was CA with Commission rules unread, and those rules (Okla. Admin. Code §§ 165:35-21-2, 165:35-21-10, 165:35-21-20 and the gas and water equivalents) supply the answer. Rows edu-utility-resale-cap-ok, edu-utility-apportionment-ok and edu-utility-shutoff-rules-ok follow the corpus.
- (S07) Brief wording (known-shapes.md item 5 and the S07 launch note): Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-4-1 does not itself say the IFC 2024 applies "statewide for existing buildings"; it adopts the IFC 2024 'as the statewide minimum code for residential and commercial fire prevention and fire protection systems within the State of Oklahoma' (Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-4-1(a)). The reach to existing buildings comes from the adoption of IFC Chapter 11, "Construction Requirements for Existing Buildings", with modifications (Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-4-16) and of unmodified provisions 'without modification' (Okla. Admin. Code § 748:20-4-4(a)). S07 rows cite those sections for the existing-building point.
- (S07) Declined to tag `common-area-lighting-ks` for Oklahoma: its sentence that the landlord "shall exercise reasonable care in the maintenance of the shared areas" states the Kansas standard, while Oklahoma requires the landlord to 'keep all common areas of his building, grounds, facilities and appurtenances in a clean, safe and sanitary condition' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(A)(1)). Wrote `common-area-lighting-ok` with the same mechanics and Oklahoma's duty.
- (S07) Pool-safety: where the Health Department rule exempts only homeowners' association pools of 'thirty or fewer homes' (Okla. Admin. Code § 310:320-1-3(b)) but the 2024 statute says the Department 'shall not regulate' 'Pools run by a homeowner's association where pools or spas are limited to use by the homeowner group and their nonpaying guests' (Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1-1022(A)) with no size limit, `edu-pool-safety-ok` states the statute's exclusion. The rule's bracketed citation is to Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 1-1013, which the corpus shows as "Repealed by Laws 2024, c. 107, § 2, eff. Nov. 1, 2024." (catchline).
- (S07) Declined to tag `waterbed-mn` for Oklahoma: its sentence "Tenant is responsible for damage the item causes" carries no fault limb, and whether a no-fault damage promise is an indemnification within Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(4) is case law not read. Wrote `waterbed-ok` with the same mechanics and the deliberate-or-negligent limb and person list of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(5) and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E).
- (S07) Declined to tag `appliances-excluded-la` for Oklahoma: its initials line and carve-outs rest on Louisiana's warranty-waiver articles (see its LA notes), and whether an item excluded by the lease escapes the duty to maintain appliances 'supplied or required to be supplied by him' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(A)(3)) is Oklahoma case law not read. `edu-appliances-excluded-ok` records the option as not offered (rule 54).
- (S07) Declined to tag `pest-cooperation-pr` and `pest-cooperation-la` for Oklahoma: their tenant-fault sentences omit the animal or pet limb and use fault and household rather than Oklahoma's 'deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant, a member of his or her family, his or her animal or pet or other person or animal on the premises with his or her consent' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E)). Wrote `pest-cooperation-ok` with Oklahoma's words.
- (S08) environmental-event-termination (OK): offered a new optional clause `environmental-event-termination-ok` (landlord may end the lease by written notice when a government authority orders the unit vacated or declares it unfit or unsafe for an environmental or public-health condition the landlord did not cause by failing its obligations), on the reasoning that produced `casualty-termination-ok` in the 2026-09-30 pass: Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111(C) lets the parties agree how a definite term ends, and the clause keeps the tenant's rights under Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121 and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122, so Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1) is not engaged. Fire and physical casualty stay with `casualty-termination-ok`.
- (S08) eminent-domain (OK): shared clause `eminent-domain-vi` not tagged for Oklahoma. No Oklahoma statute bars it (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111(C); Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1) not engaged), but its award sentence ("Any award for the taking of the property, including any value of this Lease, belongs to Landlord") sits beside Okla. Stat. tit. 27, § 14(B)-(C), which pays a tenant for tenant-owned improvements only if the owner disclaims them, and the treatment of a residential leasehold in the award is case law not read. Kept consistent with S05's `edu-condemned-premises-rent-ok`, which records a condemnation clause as lawful but not offered and asks Taylor. If Taylor chooses to offer one, `eminent-domain-vi` is the candidate, with its carve-out widened to "any payment to Tenant for Tenant's own improvements, moving costs or personal property".
- (S08) cold-weather-vacate-notice (OK): shared `cold-weather-vacate-notice-wy` not tagged. Its carve-out ("does not apply where Landlord has agreed to supply or maintain the heating system and the failure is Landlord's to repair") ties the landlord's exception to an agreement, while Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(A)(3) imposes the duty to maintain supplied heating facilities by law; read with its freeze-damage sentence the clause could shift to the tenant damage resulting from the landlord's omission in maintenance, which Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(4) makes unenforceable (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(B)). Decline on the basis check (rule 26).
- (S08) eviction-service-party (OK): no lease clause naming an agent to accept eviction papers for the tenant is offered. Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 2004(C)(1)(c)(1) recognizes service on 'an agent authorized by appointment or by law to receive service of process' (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 2004(C)(1)(c)(1)), and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A) does not list such a term, but a pre-dispute appointment inside a landlord's form lease is untested in anything read (no case read), the statutory methods of Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.5 and Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.5A suffice, and a defective appointment would put the judgment at risk; recorded as lawful but not offered (rule 54) in `edu-eviction-service-party-ok`.
- (S09) Lead disagreement (OK log §18.2 lead for hazardous-contamination-disclosure: "Meth only"): the corpus also holds a copy duty to persons in possession for recorded environmental covenants, 'Each person in possession of the real property subject to the covenant;' (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 49.17(A)(3)), and recorded remediation notices that run with the land (Okla. Stat. tit. 27A, § 2-7-123(D)-(E)); answered by edu-hazardous-contamination-disclosure-ok and the optional environmental-covenant-ok.
- (S09) Lead disagreement (OK log §18.2 lead for lease-term-limitation: "No maximum residential term; statute of frauds and recording only"): the corpus also requires both spouses to sign a homestead lease for more than one year, 'No deed, mortgage, or contract affecting the homestead exempt by law, except a lease for a period not exceeding one (1) year, shall be valid unless in writing and subscribed by both husband and wife' (Okla. Stat. tit. 16, § 4(A)), and temporary renting does not end homestead character (Okla. Stat. tit. 31, § 2(E)); answered by edu-lease-term-limits-ok.
- (S09) Lead disagreement (OK log §18.2 leads for required-disclosures and statutory-forms): two disclosures no OK row states were found: a licensee-owner must disclose licensure in writing on all transaction documents and put the license number on lease contracts (Okla. Admin. Code § 605:10-9-4(d)(1)), and each party must confirm the broker-duties disclosure 'in a separate provision, incorporated in or attached to the contract to purchase, lease, option, or exchange real estate' (Okla. Stat. tit. 59, § 858-356). New CONDITIONAL REQUIRED_DISCLOSURE clauses licensee-status-disclosure-ok and broker-duties-confirmation-ok.
- (S09) Lead disagreement (OK log §18.2 lead for military-air-zone-disclosure: "Battery 60: no disclosure statute"): still no disclosure statute (G240), but the row also states municipal compatible-use zoning (Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 43-101.1) and the SB 893 (2026) rental bar on foreign principals within ten miles of military bases, effective July 1, 2027 (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 128.3, history line "Added by Laws 2026, c. 428, § 4, eff. July 1, 2027").
- (S09) Lease-option clause declined (rule 54): a lease with an option to purchase a one- or two-unit home is a 'Contract' under the Residential Property Condition Disclosure Act (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 832(7)), and crediting rent to the price makes it a 'Sale of an interest in land' under the Consumer Credit Code (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-105(6)). When the disclosure statement is due in a lease-option is unsettled (no case read); edu-optional-lease-terms-ok gives the cautious course (deliver before signing) and offers no clause.
- (S09) Tagged foreclosure-disclosure-nv OK as an optional written record: no Oklahoma statute requires the disclosure (G258), and the record answers fraud or deceit claims based on suppression of a known fact (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 58(3); Okla. Stat. tit. 76, § 3(3)); whether silence is actionable is case law, not read.
- (S10) S10 quote-limb review: the nine passages quotelimb.py still flags were each read against the source; none drops a limb the row relies on. Five end where the next list item or an unrelated alternative begins (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 58(4); Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 765.3; Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(C)(2); Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 128(A); the sale-of-land alternative in Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 136(4), quoted twice), two have the alternative stated in the next words of the note (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 115(C)(2); the 25,000-consumer test of SB 546 (2026), section 15), and one is followed by an unrelated list item (SB 546 (2026), section 16, paragraph 17).
- (S10) S10: `scheduled-services-entry-la` was not tagged OK because its notice period is a hand-filled bracket that could be set below the one day's notice of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 128(C); `scheduled-services-entry-ok` writes the one day and reasonable times in. `extended-absence-notice-ks`, `emergency-contact-co` and `automatic-renewal-ks` were tagged as contract terms after the two-way basis check.
- (S10) S10: no clause designating who performs a tenant's repair-and-deduct work, and no clause letting a landlord end the lease when repairs cost too much, is offered for Oklahoma; each would narrow a remedy or duty a lease may not waive (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1)) and no Oklahoma case was read. Education rows record the declines (rule 54).
- (S10) S10 sweep for known shapes 19-29 (coordinator notes): fixed shape 22 (the "unless otherwise agreed" lead-in of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 119(C) restored in `edu-written-notice-required-ok` and `edu-adverse-proceeding-notice-ok`), shape 27 (supplementary battery G347 for the renewal-deadline, format and opt-in items in `edu-no-automatic-renewal-rule-ok`; topic object withdrawn and replaced) and shape 29 (the tenant's own 30- and 7-day termination notice added to `edu-written-notice-required-ok`; the electronic-transactions refusal right stated for either party in `edu-actual-notice-method-ok`). Shapes 19-21, 23-26 and 28 found nothing in S10 rows: no small-claims or supersedeas-as-condition statement (the escrow row says the tenant "may post" the bond), no abandonment rule applied to termination, no seller-disclosure act, no safe harbor, and every quotation is credited to the section that says it.
- (S11) construction-liens: the shared clause `no-liens-ar` is not tagged OK because its sentence that Tenant "will pay Landlord's reasonable costs caused by it" can be read as a promise to pay attorney fees, barred by Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(3) ('Agrees to pay the other party's attorney's fees' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(3))); `no-liens-ok` is the same clause without the cost sentence, with the release tied to the statutory cash-or-bond discharge (Okla. Stat. tit. 42, § 147.1).
- (S11) hoa: `association-approval-la` is not tagged OK. Its termination sentence ("either party may end this Lease by written notice") could cut off the tenant's Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 120(A) choice to demand performance and recover actual damages when possession is not delivered, and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1) bars waiving remedies under the Act; no Oklahoma case was read. `edu-hoa-ok` records the option as lawful in principle but not offered, and that a landlord may add its own clause after advice (rule 54).
- (S11) ev-charging-requirements: the shared `ev-charger-installation-hi` is not tagged OK (above-cost electricity reimbursement where Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 161.1(A) may cap resale of municipal power; a reference to a deposit legal limit Oklahoma lacks; a no-fault repair promise, known shape 17); `ev-charger-installation-ok` is offered instead with at-cost reimbursement and an itemized bill, and an end-of-term choice framed against Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 334.
- (S12) informal-dispute-resolution: the shared clause informal-dispute-resolution-or is not tagged OK because its notice-and-meet step is a condition before any non-possession court action, and Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 216 voids a contract condition restricting enforcement 'by the usual legal proceedings in the ordinary tribunals' (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 216); whether a short step counts is case law, not read. Offered instead: informal-dispute-resolution-ok, an expressly non-conditional clause.
- (S12) Shared rows declined for OK this slice (basis check both ways, rule 26): informal-dispute-resolution-or (a pre-suit condition at risk under Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 216 and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1)); parking-rules-notice-co (omits the written-consent condition of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 126(B)); drug-free-housing-ca (bans all cannabis use, against Okla. Stat. tit. 63, § 427.8(C)); prohibited-acts-renter-wy (narrower than Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(5) and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 128(A)). Tagged: guaranty-as and animal-care-access-vi. OK replacements offered: informal-dispute-resolution-ok and prohibited-acts-renter-ok (both optional, SERVES_LANDLORD).
- (S12) Lead disagreement (leads-2026-09-30, OK log 18.2 table): tenant-security-cameras was recorded as CA with "Battery 104: 0 hits", but G380 and the heading screen find general Oklahoma law that governs tenant cameras: one-party consent to interception (Okla. Stat. tit. 13, § 176.4(5)), voyeurism (Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 1171(B)), camera tampering by 'any unauthorized person' (Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 1993(A)) and the licensing exemptions (Okla. Stat. tit. 59, § 1800.3(2)-(3)). No camera-specific rental statute exists, so the old absence holds only in that narrow sense; edu-tenant-security-cameras-ok records both.

#### Questions asked of Taylor

- (S02) SB 546 (2026) (2026 Okla. Sess. Laws ch. 8), a consumer data privacy act effective January 1, 2027, applies to a controller or processor that handles personal data of at least 100,000 Oklahoma consumers in a calendar year (or 25,000 plus over half of revenue from selling data). edu-tenant-data-breach-ok tells landlords only the threshold and date. Steinoak itself, as software that processes landlords' applicant and tenant data, may reach the processor threshold at scale; that is a company compliance question, not a library row. Recommendation: no further library row; route SB 546 to Steinoak's own privacy/compliance review before January 1, 2027.
- (S09) Builder inputs for two new conditional required disclosures (corrected after check round 1 part E): licensee-status-disclosure-ok applies when (a) an Oklahoma real estate licensee (active or inactive) owns the property, directly or through an entity, and the rental is handled by the licensee or marketed through a firm (Okla. Admin. Code § 605:10-9-4(d)(1)), or (b) a licensee signs the lease for the owner under a power of attorney, whether or not a firm is involved (Okla. Admin. Code § 605:10-9-4(d)(3)); broker-duties-confirmation-ok applies when a broker provides brokerage services in the lease (Okla. Stat. tit. 59, § 858-356). Recommendation: add yes/no questions to the Oklahoma lease builder ("Does a real estate licensee own the property and handle or market it through a firm?", "Is a licensee signing under a power of attorney?", "Is a real estate broker involved?") and include each clause only on a yes. Non-blocking: both clauses are already CONDITIONAL in the library.

**Taylor's answers (2026-11-13):**
- OSCN Turnstile challenge (Rules for District Courts): Taylor tried the verification in the built-in browser and it failed ("oscn won't verify, gives a failure"). No workaround was attempted. Rows that need a District Court rule name it and stay NEEDS_REVIEW (SOP Part 5: a primary source that can't be reached).

### §5 Independent check

**Method (rule 80, scaled).** Independent checkers who did not write the rows checked them against the primary sources only, one checker at a time, from a frozen copy of the delta, under `work/brief/CHECK-BRIEF.md`. Each checker's errata were dry-run against the current rows and then applied once by `tools/errata.py`, which appends a replacement object marked with the erratum id. Recurring shapes went into `work/brief/known-shapes.md`, which every later helper read, and were swept across the earlier rows by the main pass.

**Round 1, part A (slices S01-S03 and the main-pass items; 67 rows, 4 proposed rows, §6 findings).** 30 errata: 10 ERROR, 17 FIX, 3 REPRO, all applied. Main-pass follow-ups M-E1 to M-E4. Shapes 1-6 in known-shapes.md came from this part. The main pass swept every earlier OK row for shapes 1 and 4; two defects in existing rows went to §6 (`edu-tenant-repair-remedies-ok`, `edu-tenant-caused-damage-ok`).

**Round 1, part B (slices S04-S05; 48 rows: 45 new, 3 tags; the S04 and S05 §6 findings).** 18 errata: 3 ERROR (B-E2 VAWA notice also due on denial of residency; B-E3 the state single-family fair-housing exemption is conditional; B-E11 casualty termination's one-week written notice and the "if continued occupancy is possible" condition), 14 FIX, 1 REPRO (G153 re-run as G199 on the grown fetched set: 5 units, conclusion unchanged); all applied. 3 NOTEs, resolved by the main pass:
- the restoration escrow may also be a deposit: the clause now places it at a federally insured institution in Oklahoma, meeting both rules (M-E40, M-E41, M-E45);
- Okla. Stat. tit. 59, § 1151.9 is compiled in its January 1, 2027 text: rows now say the roofing exemption is confirmed from that date and the earlier text was not read (M-E42 to M-E44, M-E46);
- the S05 finding on `edu-servicemember-rights-ok` suggested wording that dropped the military-service, residence and rent-cap limits: the finding was restated with the checker's wording, and `edu-eviction-hardship-stay-ok` was brought into line (M-E48).
Shapes 7-13 were added to known-shapes.md and sent to the running helpers (S07, S08). Main-pass sweeps over S01-S06: future-effective compiled text (every cited section whose History shows an effective date after 2026-11-13; all but the roofing exemption were battery hit-list mentions, not stated rules); deeming-rule wording (the ADU tag note fixed, M-E47); unconditional exemptions (the main pass then judged the owner-occupied four-family exemption in Okla. Stat. tit. 25, § 1453(C)(1)(b) unconditional; check part G showed that the list's lead-in carries the no-broker and no-notice conditions, and the rows were corrected, M-E98 to M-E100); "limited to" lists (none left); quotes ending before an or/unless/if limb (`tools/quotelimb.py` flagged 87, almost all list items whose next item is a separate limb; the eight with substantive limbs were opened and each row already states the limb).

**Round 1, part C (slices S06-S07; 41 rows: 38 new, 5 of them clauses, 3 tags; the S06 §6 finding).** 25 errata: 3 ERROR (the tariff condition cut from Okla. Admin. Code § 165:65-9-7(f) in `edu-utility-apportionment-ok`, body and notes; the mandatory lifeguard for pools with diving boards or platforms over one meter, Okla. Admin. Code § 310:320-3-2(c)(1), in `edu-pool-safety-ok`), 22 FIX; all applied. 6 NOTEs, resolved by the main pass (decision in §4): the `furnishings-included-la` tag was withdrawn and replaced by `furnishings-included-ok` with the fault limb; `utility-transfer-ok` keeps its no-shutoff promise; the 10% resale cap's reading is stated (M-E53); the energy-data tag says its self-limits are optional (M-E54); the apportionment row's search bounds name the DEQ chapters (M-E55); the fire-code vacancy paraphrase carries its limiter (M-E56, M-E57). Two checker edits needed form fixes (a quote with embedded double quotes in `edu-smoke-drift-ok`, M-E52; a library term in single quotes in a body, M-E58). Shapes 14-18 were added to known-shapes.md and sent to the running helpers (S08, S09).

**Round 1, part D (slice S08; 24 rows: 23 new, 2 of them clauses, 1 tag; the S08 §6 findings and §7 entries).** 23 errata: 8 ERROR (6 distinct defects: small claims jury rights and the abandonment rule widened to a termination for breach in `edu-landlord-remedies-termination-ok`; the supersedeas bond stated as a condition of appeal in `edu-forfeiture-redemption-ok`; dropped limbs in the prostitution-letting crime, the Act's exclusions lead-in and the agency-approved price-increase exception), 15 FIX (including the "to the extent caused by" carve-out wording in `cold-weather-vacate-notice-ok` and stale "proposed to Taylor" wording); all applied. 6 NOTEs, resolved by the main pass: a one-year cautious course for statutory double-rent claims (M-E67, M-E68); "including" before the service list (M-E69); service by an Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111(E) method for the owner-occupancy notice (M-E70, M-E71); the §7 housekeeping entry withdrawn as resolved; two checker screens recorded as support. Shapes 19-23 were added to known-shapes.md and sent to the running helpers (S10, S11); the main-pass sweep of S01-S09 found one more instance (the small-claims jury sentence in `edu-jury-waiver-ok`, M-E66).
