### §6 Findings on existing rows

- `default-by-tenant-ks-ne` (MAIN): Item 8 decision for Oklahoma (OK log §10 flag): PROPOSED OVERRIDE, apply at sync. The shared clause's remedies sentence lets Landlord recover "reasonable costs and expenses". A rental agreement may not provide that either party 'Agrees to pay the other party's attorney's fees' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(3)), and such a provision 'is unenforceable' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(B)). The costs and expenses of a default can be read to include the landlord's costs of enforcing the lease, attorney fees among them; no Oklahoma case deciding whether such a phrase is a fee promise was read, so the answer turns only on case law and the cautious course drops the phrase, as default-by-tenant-co does (its prevailing-party fee sentence is not carried over: Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(3) bars it). Sync action: remove OK from default-by-tenant-ks-ne's states (leave its OK note segment as history) and add this row. Proposed row: `default-by-tenant-ok` (gaps log §6, proposed rows), every field given; effective_from and last_checked 2026-11-13, verification_status VERIFIED, states OK.
- `tenant-maintenance` (MAIN): Item 8 decision for Oklahoma (OK log §10 flag): PROPOSED OVERRIDE, apply at sync. The shared clause asks more than the Act: it requires Tenant to keep "the property" in a "clean, safe, and sanitary condition" and to "maintain the property in the same condition as it was delivered", while the statutory duty is to keep 'that part of the premises which such tenant occupies and uses as safe, clean and sanitary as the condition of the premises permits' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(1)), and an agreement that 'the tenant is to perform specified repairs, maintenance tasks, alterations or remodeling' must be 'by a conspicuous writing independent of the rental agreement' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(B)). A lease term that conflicts with the Act is unenforceable to that extent (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 103(B)). The OK note segment's qualifier sentence sits only in notes, which the product never shows (rule 26 [NV-gaps 1]). Sync action: remove OK from tenant-maintenance's states (leave its OK segment as history) and add this row. Proposed row: `tenant-maintenance-ok` (gaps log §6, proposed rows), every field given; effective_from and last_checked 2026-11-13, verification_status VERIFIED, states OK.
- `early-termination-ks` (MAIN): Item 9(a) for Oklahoma: PROPOSED OVERRIDE, apply at sync (kickoff: don't ask Taylor). early-termination-ks charges "one month's Rent ... or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater". In Oklahoma 'penalties imposed by contract for any non-performance thereof, are void' except as Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 215 provides (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 213), and an amount fixed in advance for a breach is valid only 'when, from the nature of the case, it would be impracticable or extremely difficult to fix the actual damage' (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 215(A)). A landlord's loss when a tenant leaves early is measured by statute: the landlord must try to re-rent and the tenant owes 'the entire rent or the difference in rental, whichever may be appropriate, for the remainder of the term' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 129(B)). Whether a fee for exercising an agreed option is within those sections at all, and how a court would measure it, is case law not read, so the fee rests only on case law; the greater-of formula grows with the remaining term, the shape most exposed to the penalty rule. Sync action: remove OK from early-termination-ks's states (leave its OK segment as history) and add this row. Proposed row: `early-termination-ok` (gaps log §6, proposed rows), every field given; effective_from and last_checked 2026-11-13, verification_status VERIFIED, states OK.
- `tenant-caused-damage-ok` (MAIN): Item 9(c): PROPOSED WORDING, apply at sync. Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122(A) withholds the tenant's casualty rights where the impairment 'is caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant, a member of his family, his animal or pet or other person or animal on the premises with his consent' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122(A)). casualty-termination-ok follows that list ("a member of Tenant's family"), but tenant-caused-damage-ok says "a member of Tenant's family or household". Rule 54 [UT circle-back 2] wants the persons whose fault counts identical in both clauses and covering the statute's own list. A household member who is not family is already covered as "another person ... on the premises with Tenant's consent", so the word adds nothing and breaks the match. In tenant-caused-damage-ok's bodyText replace "a member of Tenant's family or household, Tenant's animal or pet" with "a member of Tenant's family, Tenant's animal or pet". casualty-termination-ok needs no change. Read section-open 2026-11-13: Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122.
- `edu-tenant-repair-remedies-ok` (MAIN): Round 1 shape sweep (main pass). Two defects in bodyText. (1) Dropped fault limb: the body says no right arises "if the tenant, a family member, a pet or a person or animal there with the tenant's consent caused the problem", but the statute excludes only a condition 'caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant, a member of his or her family, his or her animal or pet or other person or animal on the premises with his or her consent' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E)). Replace with "if the tenant, a family member, a pet or a person or animal there with the tenant's consent caused the problem deliberately or negligently". (2) The 72-hour cure omits an alternative and the payment method: the claim may be made by 'written notice of said claim or actual notice to the landlord's agent for collecting rent' within ten days (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.10B(A)), and payment is 'by cash or cashier's check, within seventy-two (72) hours' (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.10B(B)). Replace "who gave you written notice within 10 days after the rent was due, may cure within 72 hours of judgment by paying the judgment, costs and fees" with "who gave you written notice of the claim, or actual notice to your agent who collects rent, within 10 days after the rent was due, may cure within 72 hours of judgment by paying the judgment, court costs and attorney fees in cash or by cashier's check". Read section-open 2026-11-13.
- `edu-tenant-caused-damage-ok` (MAIN): Round 1 shape sweep (main pass). Dropped fault limb, in the landlord's favour: the body says Oklahoma's casualty, repair-and-deduct, essential-service and habitability remedies are each unavailable "when the tenant, a family member, a pet or a person or animal there with the tenant's consent caused the condition". The statutes take those remedies away only where the condition was caused 'by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of' the tenant's side (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E); Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122(A)); an accident the tenant did not cause deliberately or negligently leaves the tenant's remedies in place. Replace "caused the condition" with "caused the condition deliberately or negligently", and "a tenant who causes a fire" with "a tenant who causes a fire deliberately or negligently". edu-casualty-ok states the limb correctly. Read section-open 2026-11-13.
- `edu-foreign-ownership-ok` (MAIN): Supplement to the S09 finding (raised by check round 1 part E, confirmed by the main pass): the compiled Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 121(B), as amended by HB 3431 (2026) (2026 Okla. Sess. Laws ch. 301, history line "Laws 2026, c. 301, § 1, eff. Nov. 1, 2026"), now also reaches 'any person who is not a citizen of the United States' (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 121(B)) and critical minerals, and ends with a proviso the row's body does not state: 'the requirements of this subsection shall not apply to a business entity that is engaged in regulated interstate commerce or has a national security agreement with the Committee on Foreign Investment in the United States (CFIUS) in accordance with federal law' (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 121(B)). The bona fide resident exception the body states is in Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 122. Suggested addition to the body, after the November 1, 2026 sentence: "The ban does not apply to a business entity engaged in regulated interstate commerce or one with a national security agreement with the federal Committee on Foreign Investment in the United States." No other change; whether a residential lease is "leasing land" remains unread case law, as the row says.
- `landlord-maintenance` (MAIN): OK segment of the tagged shared clause (S10 finding, corrected by the main pass after check round 1 part F). The clause relieves Landlord of repairs "where repair is necessary due to improper use by Tenant or a guest of Tenant", and its OK note says "the improper-use exception matches Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E)". It does not: Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E) withholds the tenant's remedies only where the condition 'was caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant, a member of his or her family, his or her animal or pet or other person or animal on the premises with his or her consent' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E)). Proposed fix: the override `landlord-maintenance-ok` (see Proposed rows), which keeps "located at the property", adds the fault-limited cost sentence and the savings sentence from `landlord-maintenance-nd`, and removes OK from `landlord-maintenance`.
- `edu-fair-housing-ok` (MAIN): Owner-occupied exemption stated without its lead-in (check round 1 part G shape, confirmed by the main pass). The row says Oklahoma's fair housing law does not apply "to rooms or units in a building of up to four families where the owner lives in one of them". That exemption is one item of a list that opens 'Subject to division (2) of subparagraph a of this paragraph, Sections 1451 through 1453 of this title do not apply to:' (Okla. Stat. tit. 25, § 1453(C)(1)), and division (2) is the condition that the house was rented without a licensed broker's services and without 'the publication, posting, or mailing of a notice, statement, or advertisement prohibited by Section 1452 of this title' (Okla. Stat. tit. 25, § 1453(C)(1)(a)(2)(b)). Suggested wording: "to rooms or units in a building of up to four families where the owner lives in one of them (treat the no-broker and no-discriminatory-advertising conditions as applying here too)". This pass's rows `edu-children-occupancy-ok`, `edu-portfolio-thresholds-ok` and `edu-no-protected-class-inquiry-ban-ok` state it that way.
- `edu-no-nuisance-eviction-rule-ok` (MAIN): Second defect, no change made (from check round 2 part 2): the row's last sentence, "Oklahoma's general nuisance statutes were not read for owner liability", and its notes' "Title 50 not read whole" are now stale: Okla. Stat. tit. 50, §§ 1-44 were read whole on 2026-11-13 for `edu-no-expedited-criminal-eviction-ok`. Suggested replacement sentence: "Under Oklahoma's general nuisance statute, a later owner who neglects to abate a continuing nuisance created by a former owner is liable for it in the same way as the one who created it; how far a landlord answers for a nuisance a tenant creates is case law not reviewed here." Source: 'Every successive owner of property who neglects to abate a continuing nuisance upon, or in the use of such property, created by a former owner, is liable therefor in the same manner as the one who first created it.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 50, § 5). Replace the notes' "Title 50 not read whole" with "Okla. Stat. tit. 50, §§ 1-44 read whole 2026-11-13". Both defects can be fixed in one edit, together with the gambling-lease sentence in the finding above.
- `edu-consumer-protection-ok` (S01): Currency only, no change to the body needed: the row's notes cite Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 754(2) as read in the 2025 compilation. SB 1448 (2026), effective November 1, 2026 ('This act shall become effective November 1, 2026.' (SB 1448 (2026))), renumbered the exemptions; the compiled section now lists the Corporation Commission exemption at (2), 'Actions or transactions required or authorized under specific laws or regulations administered by any other regulatory body or officer acting under statutory authority of this state or the United States' (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 754(3)), a separate good-faith exemption at (4) and the tax exemption at (5). The body's sentence "Transactions regulated by another regulatory body are exempt" still fits (3). Suggest the notes add "Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 754(2)-(3), as amended by SB 1448 (2026), eff. Nov. 1, 2026" at sync.
- `tenant-repair-agreement-ok` (S01): Style only: the clause body opens with a bracketed drafting note that carries a statute citation, "(Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(B))"; the brief's clause rule is lease text with no statute citations. Suggest at sync moving the citation to notes and keeping the bracket as "[Optional. Oklahoma requires this agreement to be a conspicuous writing independent of the rental agreement: print it as its own document, signed separately by Landlord and Tenant, not as a section or addendum of the Lease.]". Separately, consider whether the agreement should state the consideration the tenant receives (general contract law: 'Sufficient cause or consideration.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 2)); it currently names none. No legal error in the statutory content.
- `edu-legal-interest-ok` (S03): Reason for the decline has changed (gaps log §4, class decision 9 asks for a finding when the old reason no longer holds); the decline itself can stand. The row's body says the parties may agree to 'any rate as may be authorized by law' (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 266) "but the laws that limit an agreed rate were not reviewed for this library". Read 2026-11-13: the Constitution deems usurious 'all contracts for a greater rate of interest than ten percent(10%) per annum' only 'in the absence of legislation fixing maximum rates of interest' (Okla. Const. art. XIV, § 2), with forfeiture of 'the entire interest' and recovery of 'twice the amount of the interest so paid' for knowing usury (Okla. Const. art. XIV, § 3). The Consumer Credit Code fixes rates for loans and credit sales, but 'Loan' reaches 'the forbearance of debt arising from a loan' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 3-106(1)(d)) and its other limbs are lender advances and card debt, while a 'consumer credit sale' is 'a sale of goods, services or an interest in land' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-104(1)); an overdue-rent debt is neither. So no statute fixes a maximum for interest agreed on lease debts, and whether the constitutional ten-percent ceiling governs is case law (unread). Proposed body at sync: "When a lease sets no interest rate, Oklahoma's legal rate of interest is 6% a year. The parties may agree by contract to any rate the law authorizes. No statute sets a maximum rate for interest on overdue rent or other lease debts: the Consumer Credit Code's rates cover loans and credit sales. The Constitution treats a contract rate above 10% a year as usurious where no statute fixes a maximum, and knowingly charging usurious interest forfeits all the interest and lets the payer recover twice the interest paid. No court decision on interest on lease debts was reviewed, so the library offers no interest clause. A landlord who wants one can add a custom clause after advice, keeping the rate at or below 10% a year." Notes to add: Okla. Const. art. XIV, §§ 2-3; Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, §§ 2-104, 2-605, 3-106, 3-605; Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 275; constitution currency fetch pending (file dated January 4, 2022). See edu-statutory-caps-ok.
- `acceptable-payment-methods` (S03): Landlord duty not stated by any OK row, on a topic Oklahoma already answers (acceptable-payment-methods): the OK note on acceptable-payment-methods reads "No Oklahoma statute on residential rent payment methods", but Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211 (amended 2025, read whole 2026-11-13) limits credit-card surcharges by a 'Seller', defined as 'any person, entity, or retailer doing business in this state in any sales, service, or lease transaction including, but not limited to, any consumer credit sales transaction' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(E)(2)): posted notice, a cap of the lesser of 2% or actual processing cost, and no surcharge where only credit cards are accepted (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(B)). Whether it reaches residential rent is unread case law; the cautious course is to follow it. Proposed: add the education row edu-card-surcharge-ok (proposed_row object in S03.jsonl, every field filled, topic_key acceptable-payment-methods), and at sync add to the OK note on acceptable-payment-methods: "Credit card surcharges: Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211 (see edu-card-surcharge-ok)." No change to the clause body is needed: it lists methods and does not mention surcharges.
- `edu-fair-housing-ok` (S04): edu-fair-housing-ok says "You must allow reasonable modifications for a person with a disability at that person's expense (the person may be required to provide a surety bond guaranteeing restoration)". The statute limits the bond to restoration that is necessary: 'provided that such person also provides a surety bond guaranteeing restoration of the premises to their prior condition, if necessary to make the premises suitable for nondisabled tenants' (Okla. Stat. tit. 25, § 1452(A)(16)(a)); and federal law, which applies in Oklahoma, forbids raising the security deposit and allows restoration funding only by an interest-bearing escrow 'where it is necessary in order to ensure with reasonable certainty that funds will be available' (FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(a)). Suggested wording: "(where restoration will be needed to make the unit suitable for tenants without disabilities, the person may be required to provide a surety bond guaranteeing restoration; federal law limits this, see edu-disability-accommodation-ok)". Its notes also say the Federal Fair Housing Act was "not researched here"; edu-disability-accommodation-ok now carries the federal rules.
- `edu-statute-of-frauds-ok` (S05): The body says "A lease for one year or less accompanied by actual possession is valid against third persons without being acknowledged and recorded; a longer lease must be acknowledged and recorded to be valid against third persons such as a later buyer or lender." The statute's exception needs both conditions: no lease 'other than a lease for a period not exceeding one (1) year and accompanied by actual possession, shall be valid as against third persons unless acknowledged and recorded as herein provided.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 16, § 15). So a lease of one year or less whose tenant is not yet in possession (signed before move-in) is also unprotected against a buyer or lender unless recorded; the row reads as if only longer leases need recording. Suggested wording for the last sentence: "A lease for one year or less is valid against third persons without being acknowledged and recorded only once the tenant is in actual possession; any other lease (a longer one, or a short one before the tenant moves in) must be acknowledged and recorded to be valid against third persons such as a later buyer or lender." New row `edu-lease-registration-ok` (S05) states it this way.
- `edu-servicemember-rights-ok` (S05): Its notes say "Federal: 50 U.S.C. §§ 3955 (lease termination), 3951 (evictions) (rule 21; not researched here)". The main pass has since fetched FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3951 (uscode.house.gov, current through Pub. L. 119-138), and S05 read it whole for `edu-eviction-hardship-stay-ok`: 'Except by court order, a landlord (or another person with paramount title) may not' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3951(a)(1)) evict a servicemember or dependents during a period of military service from premises occupied primarily as a residence with monthly rent under the adjusted cap, and on request of a servicemember whose ability to pay is materially affected the court shall stay the proceedings for 90 days, unless justice and equity require a longer or shorter period, or adjust the obligation (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3951(b)(1)). Suggest the row's body add (wording corrected by check round 1 part B to keep the military-service condition, the residence and rent-cap limits and the court's discretion on the period): "If the tenant is a servicemember (or a servicemember's dependent), the unit is their residence and the monthly rent is under the federal limit, you need a court order to evict during military service, and the court must, on request of a servicemember whose ability to pay is materially affected by service, stay the case for 90 days (or another period the court finds just) or adjust the lease obligation." and its notes drop "not researched here" for FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3951.
- `edu-utility-resale-ok` (S06): edu-utility-resale-ok (topic utility-submetering-disclosure) says "If you buy electricity or natural gas from a municipality and resell it" and states the 10% cap and bill disclosure of Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 161.1 only. Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1 ("Added by Laws 1999, c. 212, § 6, eff. Nov. 1, 1999." (history line)) imposes the same duty for water and sewer: no owner who 'purchases water or sewage services from a municipality and who resells such water or sewage services to any residential lessee' (Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1(A)) may charge 'any amount in excess of ten percent (10%) of the cost to such reseller for each billing cycle' (Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1(A)), with per-unit-cost and fee disclosure (Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1(B)), misdemeanor and treble damages (Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1(C)), and no commercial-lease proviso. The row is not wrong but omits a landlord duty on a topic OK already answers. This pass states the water and sewer rule in new row edu-utility-resale-cap-ok (topic utility-allowance-cap); proposed at sync: add to edu-utility-resale-ok's body the sentence "The same 10% cap and bill disclosure apply if you buy water or sewer service from a city and resell it to a residential tenant (there is no commercial-lease exception for water and sewer)." and to its notes the citation Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1(A)-(D).
- `edu-holdover-ok` (S08): Omission, no change made: the row names Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111(D) and Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 69 but not the neighbouring willful-holdover measure, 'For willfully holding over real property, by a tenant after the end of his term, and after notice to quit has been duly given, and demand of possession made, the measure of damages is double the yearly value of the property, for the time of withholding, in addition to compensation for the detriment occasioned thereby.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 70), read section-open 2026-11-13 (history line "R.L. 1910, § 2881"). Suggested sentence after the row's double-rent sentence: "Another older statute makes double the yearly value of the property, for the time the tenant withholds it, plus compensation for the harm caused, the measure where a tenant willfully holds over after the term ends and after notice to quit has been duly given and possession demanded; how these older statutes fit with the Landlord and Tenant Act is unsettled." Add Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 70 to its notes beside Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 69. `edu-holdover-rate-ok` states it already.
- `edu-no-nuisance-eviction-rule-ok` (S08): Defect, no change made: the row says "no Oklahoma statute was found requiring landlords to join crime-free programs, making a residential lease void for nuisance activity, or giving a landlord a special nuisance-eviction procedure". Two criminal statutes void a lease when the premises are used for illegal gambling, which is itself a public nuisance (Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 946): 'All leases between landlords and tenants, under which any tenant shall use the premises for the purpose of violating any provisions of this act shall be wholly null and void, and the landlord may recover possession thereof, as in forcible entry and detainer.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 945), and on the lessee's conviction 'the lease or contract or letting such house or building shall become void and the lessor may enter upon the premises so let and shall recover possession of said leased property as in the case of forcible detainer' (Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 958). Suggested added sentence: "One exception: a lease is void by statute if the tenant uses the premises for illegal gambling, or is convicted of allowing prohibited gambling devices there, and you recover possession through an ordinary eviction case." Add Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 945 and Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 958 to its notes. Read section-open 2026-11-13; screened by G257. `edu-no-expedited-criminal-eviction-ok` states it.
- `edu-foreign-ownership-ok` (S09): Stale currency sentence, not a legal error. Its OK notes say "The compilation (current through the 2025 session) does not yet show either act." The corpus read on 2026-11-13 (statutes updated through November 12, 2026) now prints SB 893 (2026) act section 4 as Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 128.3, history line "Added by Laws 2026, c. 428, § 4, eff. July 1, 2027" (history line), with text matching the enrolled act, including 'An individual or entity who is not a foreign entity shall not be required to determine or inquire whether another person or entity is or may be subject to the provisions of this section, and shall bear no civil or criminal liability under the provisions of this section.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 128.3(G)). Suggested notes edit at sync: replace that sentence with "The compilation of November 12, 2026 prints Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 128.3 (eff. July 1, 2027) and Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 121 (Laws 2026, c. 301)." The body's statement of the July 1, 2027 rule matches the compiled text. Found by S09 on topic military-air-zone-disclosure.
- `no-alterations` (S11): Check of `no-alterations` against telecom, solar and EV access, restated by S11R after the federal antenna rule was read: no Oklahoma statute gives a tenant a right to install telecom equipment (G358), a plug-in or other solar device (G360) or an EV charger (G365) that the clause would forbid. The federal antenna rule does limit lease terms: it prohibits 'Any restriction, including ... lease provision ... on property within the exclusive use or control of the antenna user where the user has a direct or indirect ownership or leasehold interest in the property that impairs the installation, maintenance, or use of' (FEDERAL: 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000(a)(1)) covered dishes, antennas and masts, 'to the extent it so impairs, subject to paragraph (b) of this section.' (FEDERAL: 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000(a)(1)), and a restriction impairs if it 'Unreasonably delays or prevents installation, maintenance, or use;' (FEDERAL: 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000(a)(3)(i)). Read alone, the clause's first sentence ("Tenant will not perform any alterations or improvements to the property, including adding, changing, or removing appliances, fixtures, shelving, wallpaper, or paint, without the prior written consent of Landlord.") could reach a dish mounted on a balcony or patio the tenant alone uses, and a consent requirement that unreasonably delays or prevents that installation is barred to that extent. The clause's last sentence ("This Section does not limit any repair, installation, rekeying, or reasonable modification that applicable law entitles Tenant to perform") defers to applicable law, which includes the federal rule, so no Oklahoma conflict remains and no change is required. Optional clarity edit (a central shared-text change, Taylor's call under rule 62): the rule is framed as a bar on restrictions rather than an entitlement, so the last sentence could read "This Section does not limit any repair, installation, rekeying, or reasonable modification that applicable law entitles Tenant to perform or protects from restriction (for example, a satellite dish or antenna covered by federal rules), and does not change who owns an installation that applicable law makes Tenant's property." Solar: a tenant who connects generation in parallel with a Commission-regulated utility needs 'prior written approval by the utility of such parallel operation and for the interconnection facilities' (Okla. Admin. Code § 165:35-29-2(b)) unless an exception in Okla. Admin. Code § 165:35-29-2(a) applies (other utilities, parties under Okla. Admin. Code § 165:35-29-1 who have not agreed to operate under this section, and certain pre-1981 agreements); that supports rather than conflicts with the clause. Recommendation: no change needed; `edu-telecom-access-ok` now states the federal antenna rule for Oklahoma landlords.
- `pet-policy-ok` (S12): Known shape 17 (no-fault promise) in `pet-policy-ok`: its body says "Tenant is responsible for all damage, waste removal, odor and disturbance caused by a pet." with no fault limb, while the tenant's statutory duty is to 'Not deliberately or negligently destroy, deface, damage, impair or remove any part of the premises or permit any person, animal or pet to do so' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(5)), and the Act's remedy exceptions turn on a condition 'caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant, a member of his or her family, his or her animal or pet or other person or animal on the premises with his or her consent' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E)). No section of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A) expressly bars a tenant promise of this kind, and no Oklahoma case was read on it; flagged only because the independent check treats no-fault damage promises as not stating Oklahoma law (known shape 17). Suggested wording (corrected after check round 1 part G, which found that the first suggestion put a fault limb on waste removal and disturbance, duties Oklahoma imposes on the tenant without fault: 'Dispose from such tenant's dwelling unit all ashes, garbage, rubbish and other waste in a safe, clean and sanitary manner' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(2)) and 'Not engage in conduct or allow any person or animal or pet, on the premises with the express or implied permission or consent of the tenant, to engage in conduct that will disturb the quiet and peaceful enjoyment of the premises by other tenants' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(6))): "Tenant will remove all pet waste and will not allow a pet to disturb other tenants. Tenant is responsible for damage and odor caused by a pet to the extent caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, a member of Tenant's family, Tenant's animal or pet, or another person or animal on the premises with Tenant's consent." Found by S12 while answering animal-care-access; not edited.

#### Proposed rows (every field Claude Code needs; states OK, is_active TRUE, verification_status VERIFIED, effective_from and last_checked 2026-11-13)

**`default-by-tenant-ok`** — group Default & Termination | title Tenant Default | topic_key default-by-tenant | rule_type REQUIRED | content_type LEASE_CLAUSE | lease_clause_basis SERVES_LANDLORD | supersedes default-by-tenant
- bodyText: Tenant will be in default under this Lease if Tenant fails to pay Rent when due and does not cure the failure within the time period specified by applicable law after receiving written notice from Landlord, or fails to comply with any other obligation under this Lease and does not cure the failure after receiving written notice, except where applicable law permits Landlord to proceed without giving Tenant an opportunity to cure. Landlord need not give Tenant an opportunity to cure any breach, including a failure to pay Rent, where applicable law permits Landlord to proceed without one. Except as required by applicable law, Tenant's failure to pay an assessed late fee, apart from the underlying Rent itself, will not by itself entitle Landlord to terminate this Lease or pursue eviction. If Tenant is in default, Landlord may exercise all rights and remedies available under applicable law, including terminating this Lease, regaining possession of the property, and recovering unpaid Rent and late fees, less amounts obtained from the Security Deposit. Landlord will use reasonable efforts to mitigate damages resulting from Tenant's default to the extent required by applicable law.
- notes: OK: Overrides default-by-tenant and replaces Oklahoma's tag on default-by-tenant-ks-ne (gaps log §6). Same text as default-by-tenant-ks-ne except that the remedies sentence drops "and reasonable costs and expenses", which could be read as an agreement to pay the landlord's attorney's fees, barred by Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(3) and unenforceable under Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(B); no Oklahoma case was read on that reading, so the cautious course drops it. Nothing recoverable is lost: the prevailing party in an action for breach of a rental agreement 'shall be entitled to reasonable attorneys' fees' by statute (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 105(B); edu-attorney-fees-ok). Cure: nonpayment, five days after written demand (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 131(B)); other breaches, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 132(B); no-cure grounds Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 132(C) and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 132(D), reached by the carve-out; the separate cure sentence was vouched for Oklahoma on 2026-10-03 (OK log, Retro checks (SOP 1.49)). Mitigation: Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 105(A), Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 129(B). Read section-open 2026-11-13 from the OK corpus (oklegislature.gov whole-title files, statutes updated through November 12, 2026): Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 105, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 129, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 131, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 132. Three-bucket: SERVES_LANDLORD.

**`tenant-maintenance-ok`** — group Tenant Responsibilities | title Tenant Maintenance & Cleanliness | topic_key tenant-maintenance | rule_type RECOMMENDED | content_type LEASE_CLAUSE | lease_clause_basis SERVES_LANDLORD | supersedes tenant-maintenance
- bodyText: Tenant will keep the part of the property that Tenant occupies and uses as safe, clean and sanitary as the condition of the property permits; will dispose of all ashes, garbage, rubbish and other waste from the dwelling in a safe, clean and sanitary manner; will keep all plumbing fixtures in the dwelling or used by Tenant as clean and sanitary as their condition permits; and will use all electrical, plumbing, sanitary, heating, ventilating, air-conditioning and other facilities and appliances, including any elevators, in a safe and nondestructive manner consistent with their intended purpose. Tenant will not obstruct access to doors and windows, and will not deliberately or negligently destroy, deface, damage, impair or remove any part of the property, or permit any person, animal or pet to do so. This Section does not assign Tenant any specific repair or maintenance task; any such task must be agreed in a separate written agreement, and Landlord remains responsible for the repairs Oklahoma law requires Landlord to make.
- notes: OK: Overrides tenant-maintenance (gaps log §6). Restates the tenant's statutory duties in the statute's own terms (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(1)-(5)): 'as safe, clean and sanitary as the condition of the premises permits' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(1)); waste 'in a safe, clean and sanitary manner' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(2)); plumbing fixtures 'as clean and sanitary as their condition permits' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(3)); facilities used 'in a safe and nondestructive manner' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(4)); 'Not deliberately or negligently destroy, deface, damage, impair or remove any part of the premises or permit any person, animal or pet to do so' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(5)). The shared clause's "maintain the property in the same condition as it was delivered" is dropped: a maintenance undertaking needs the separate conspicuous writing of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(B) (tenant-repair-agreement-ok), and condition at move-out is covered by surrender-end-of-term-ks-ne (tagged OK). Read section-open 2026-11-13 from the OK corpus (oklegislature.gov whole-title files, statutes updated through November 12, 2026): Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 103, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127. Three-bucket: SERVES_LANDLORD (restates tenant duties).

**`early-termination-ok`** — group Default & Termination | title Early Termination by Tenant | topic_key early-termination | rule_type CONDITIONAL | content_type LEASE_CLAUSE | lease_clause_basis SERVES_LANDLORD | supersedes early-termination-ks
- bodyText: Tenant may terminate this Lease before the end of the Term by providing Landlord at least 30 days' written notice. Tenant will pay Landlord, by the termination date, an early termination fee equal to one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}), and remains responsible for Rent and other obligations up to the termination date. If Tenant gives that notice, pays the fee and delivers possession of the property by the termination date, Tenant owes no Rent for any period after the termination date. If Tenant leaves before the end of the Term without using this option, Tenant's liability is governed by Oklahoma law. This early-termination option and fee apply only if this Lease has a fixed Term; a month-to-month or other periodic tenancy ends on the notice that applicable law and this Lease provide, without an early termination fee. Landlord may terminate this Lease early in accordance with this Lease's Tenant Default and notice provisions, or if Tenant vacates or abandons the property without notifying Landlord. Nothing in this Section limits any right either party has under applicable law, including a Tenant's right to terminate without penalty because of military service under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act, a Tenant's right as a victim of domestic violence, sexual violence or stalking to terminate without penalty, and a Tenant's right to vacate and terminate after a fire or other casualty that was not caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, a member of Tenant's family, Tenant's animal or pet, or another person or animal on the premises with Tenant's consent.
- notes: OK: Override of early-termination-ks (gaps log §6, Oklahoma item 9(a)), built on early-termination-ga and early-termination-mo. Flat one-month fee instead of the greater of one month or 30% of remaining Rent: no Oklahoma statute governs a lease early-termination fee, and a fixed amount is tested by the penalty rules, 'penalties imposed by contract for any non-performance thereof, are void' (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 213) and a stipulated amount is valid only 'when, from the nature of the case, it would be impracticable or extremely difficult to fix the actual damage' (Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 215(A)); damages 'must, in all cases, be reasonable' (Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 97); no Oklahoma case on lease early-termination fees was read, so one month's Rent is the cautious figure (edu-penalties-liquidated-damages-ok). Without the option the statutory measure applies: reasonable efforts to re-rent, then 'the entire rent or the difference in rental, whichever may be appropriate, for the remainder of the term' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 129(B)); duty to mitigate (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 105(A)). No fee may be charged to a protected victim who ends the lease under Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111(F): a victim of domestic violence, sexual violence or stalking 'may terminate a lease without penalty by providing written notice and a protective order of an incident of such violence within thirty (30) days of such incident, unless the landlord waives such time period' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111(F)); edu-dv-protections-ok. Military: 'The lessor may not impose an early termination charge' (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(e)(1)); state forces on state active duty get the same protections (Okla. Stat. tit. 44, § 208.1); edu-servicemember-rights-ok. Casualty: Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122(A) (tenant may vacate and terminate unless the impairment was caused by the tenant's side); casualty-termination-ok. Read section-open 2026-11-13 from the OK corpus (oklegislature.gov whole-title files, statutes updated through November 12, 2026): Okla. Stat. tit. 15, §§ 213-215, Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 97, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 105, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 129, Okla. Stat. tit. 44, § 208.1; FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955 read 2026-11-13 from uscode.house.gov (laws in effect through Pub. L. 119-138). Three-bucket: SERVES_LANDLORD.

**`landlord-maintenance-ok`** — group Landlord Responsibilities | title Maintenance & Repairs | topic_key landlord-maintenance | rule_type RECOMMENDED | content_type LEASE_CLAUSE | lease_clause_basis SERVES_LANDLORD | CONSTRAINED_TERM | supersedes landlord-maintenance
- bodyText: Subject to Tenant's duties under Oklahoma law and under any separate written agreement in which Tenant agrees to perform specified repairs or maintenance tasks, Landlord will maintain the property, including its structural elements, roof, and systems, in good order and repair, and will be responsible for repairing the appliances, fixtures, and equipment located at the property. Where a condition was caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, a member of Tenant's family, Tenant's animal or pet, or another person or animal on the property with Tenant's consent, Tenant will pay Landlord's reasonable cost of the repair. Tenant will notify Landlord promptly in writing of any condition requiring repair or maintenance, and Landlord will undertake required repairs within a reasonable time, consistent with applicable law. This does not limit any right applicable law gives Tenant on notice of a condition Landlord ought to repair, however that notice is given.
- notes: OK: Overrides `landlord-maintenance` for Oklahoma and replaces its OK tag (proposed by S10, corrected after check round 1 part F and round 2 part 2). Read section-open 2026-11-13 from the OK corpus (oklegislature.gov whole-title files, statutes updated through November 12, 2026): Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 125, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 132, Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.10B. Why: the shared clause excuses the landlord "where repair is necessary due to improper use by Tenant or a guest of Tenant" (library text), which has no deliberate-or-negligent limb and names fewer persons than the rule that a tenant loses repair remedies only where 'the condition complained of was caused by the deliberate or negligent act or omission of the tenant, a member of his or her family, his or her animal or pet or other person or animal on the premises with his or her consent' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E)); the landlord's duty to 'Make all repairs and do whatever is necessary to put and keep the tenant's dwelling unit and premises in a fit and habitable condition' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(A)(2)) has no tenant-fault exception and may not be waived in the rental agreement (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(1)); specified repairs and maintenance tasks can be shifted to the tenant only because 'The landlord and tenant of a dwelling unit may agree by a conspicuous writing independent of the rental agreement that the tenant is to perform specified repairs, maintenance tasks, alterations or remodeling.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(B)), which is why the opening refers to Tenant's statutory duties and any separate written agreement (`tenant-repair-agreement-ok`; class decision CD6) instead of the shared clause's "Tenant's own maintenance obligations under this Lease" (library text), which would let a term in the lease body cut back the landlord's promise. The override keeps the repair with the landlord and puts its cost on the tenant only for fault-caused conditions, matching the tenant's duty to 'Not deliberately or negligently destroy, deface, damage, impair or remove any part of the premises or permit any person, animal or pet to do so' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127(5)) and `tenant-caused-damage-ok`; the cost is not billed as rent (billing it as rent would need the written notice and cure of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 132(A)). It keeps the shared words "located at the property" so the promise does not drop facilities 'supplied or required to be supplied by him' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 118(A)(3)). The savings sentence (as in `landlord-maintenance-nd`) keeps the written-notice request from being read against the tenant's remedies on 'actual notice' to the landlord's agent for collecting rent (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1148.10B(A)) and the reporting duty of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 125. Basis: SERVES_LANDLORD (notice and cost terms) | CONSTRAINED_TERM (fault limb). Related: `edu-repair-notice-ok`, `edu-tenant-repair-remedies-ok`, `tenant-caused-damage-ok`.

**`edu-card-surcharge-ok`** — group Rent & Payment | title Credit Card Surcharges on Rent | topic_key acceptable-payment-methods | rule_type CONSTRAINED | content_type LANDLORD_EDUCATION | lease_clause_basis  | supersedes (none)
- bodyText: If you let tenants pay rent or other charges by credit card and add a surcharge for doing so, follow Oklahoma's credit card surcharge statute, which applies to anyone doing business in any sale, service or lease transaction. Post the surcharge amount clearly where the tenant pays: at the entry and point of payment for in-person payments, on the home page and the payment page for online payments, and say it aloud for payments taken by phone. The surcharge may not exceed 2% of the payment or your actual cost of processing the card payment, whichever is less. If you accept only credit cards, you may not add a surcharge at all. You may instead offer a discount for paying by cash, check or debit card, offered to every tenant clearly and conspicuously. No court decision on whether the statute reaches residential rent was reviewed, so the cautious course is to follow it. A surcharge the lease requires is also rent under the Landlord and Tenant Act.
- notes: OK: Present. Read section-open 2026-11-13 from the OK corpus (oklegislature.gov whole-title files, statutes updated through November 12, 2026): Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211, Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-106, Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 3-508C, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 102. 'No seller may impose a surcharge on a cardholder who elects to pay using a credit card instead of paying by cash, check, debit card, or similar means unless the seller complies with the following requirements' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(B)): posted notice 'at the point of entry and the point of sale for in-person transactions and the home page and the point-of-sale webpage for online transactions' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(B)(1)), and 'No surcharge shall exceed two percent (2%) of the total transaction or the actual amount to be charged to the person or retailer to process the credit card transaction, whichever is less.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(B)(2)); 'A customer shall not be considered to have chosen to use a credit card as a method of payment under this section if, at the time of the transaction, the person or retailer accepts only credit cards as payment.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(B)(2)). 'Seller' means 'any person, entity, or retailer doing business in this state in any sales, service, or lease transaction including, but not limited to, any consumer credit sales transaction' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(E)(2)); the section's history ends "Laws 2025, c. 410, § 1, eff. Nov. 1, 2025" (history line). The words "lease transaction" are not limited to goods in the section, although Title 14A's defined 'Consumer lease' is 'a lease of goods' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-106(1)); no Oklahoma case was read on whether a residential rent payment is a lease transaction under Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211, so the cautious course is to comply. A debit-card or electronic-payment convenience fee is regulated only for lenders (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 3-508C) and public bodies, not landlords. Discount: a discount 'for the purpose of inducing payment by cash, check, debit card, or similar means rather than by use of a credit card' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(A)) is not a credit service charge if offered 'to all prospective buyers clearly and conspicuously in accordance with regulations of the Administrator of Consumer Credit' (Okla. Stat. tit. 14A, § 2-211(A)); those regulations were not read. Found by G82 and G83 in the S03 tenant-portal topic. Proposed under finding_existing on `acceptable-payment-methods`, whose OK note says "No Oklahoma statute on residential rent payment methods" in library text (quoted in the finding).

### §7 Findings for other states

- (S05) Brief note versus corpus: the main pass's note to S05 said S03 "read that Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 22-110.1 bars only some local fees (registration and contact-information fees)". The section bars the registration ordinance itself: 'No municipality shall enact or attempt to enforce through fees, civil fines or criminal penalties any ordinance, rule or regulation to require the registration of real property.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 22-110.1(B)); the fee-only bar is on contact-information collection, 'Municipal governments shall not collect a fee to administer the collection of information authorized in this section.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 11, § 22-110.1(C)). S03's own rows (S03.jsonl lines 30-31) state it correctly, so only the relayed summary was narrower than the text.
- (S08) Brief versus corpus (leads-2026-09-30.md, OK log §18.2 and OK log §6.1): the lead for expedited-criminal-eviction ("§ 41-132(D) immediate termination; no special court track") and the existing row `edu-no-nuisance-eviction-rule-ok` (OK battery 91, "lease void or forfeit for illegal use: 12 sections, none a residential lease forfeiture rule") missed two statutes that void a lease for gambling use: 'All leases between landlords and tenants, under which any tenant shall use the premises for the purpose of violating any provisions of this act shall be wholly null and void, and the landlord may recover possession thereof, as in forcible entry and detainer.' (Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 945) and Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 958 (lease void on the lessee's conviction for suffering gambling devices). Found by S08's screen and confirmed by G257 (t1 hits include Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 945 and Okla. Stat. tit. 21, § 958). Recorded in `edu-no-expedited-criminal-eviction-ok` and in a finding on `edu-no-nuisance-eviction-rule-ok`.
- (S08) Brief versus corpus (leads-2026-09-30.md, OK log §6.1 holdover decline): the decline reason cites Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 111(D) and Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 69 only; the corpus also holds the willful-holdover measure 'double the yearly value of the property, for the time of withholding, in addition to compensation for the detriment occasioned thereby' (Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 70). The decline itself still holds under Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 215(A) (`edu-holdover-rate-ok`); finding filed on `edu-holdover-ok`. Also, the 2026-09-30 lead for statutory-early-termination ("AE: DV, SCRA") understates the tenant's statutory termination rights, which also include Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 120(A), Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(A), (C)(1), (D), Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122(A)(1), Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 123 and Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 124(A) (`edu-statutory-early-termination-ok`).

### Proposed SOP changes

1. (S02) Use single quotes only for statute text in every object type, including topicq, decision and question: those objects carry no id, so a quote error in them cannot be retired with replace:true (S02 lines 3 and 31).
2. (S02) Before naming a real positive, confirm the snippet is in the section BODY (show.py), not only its catchline: three S02 batteries failed on heading-only or out-of-window real positives (G7, G16, G57).
3. (S08) Run python3 -I tools/quotelimb.py <SLICE> after every topic, not only assemble.py: S08's assemble runs passed while quotelimb found ten quotes cut before an 'or' limb (list alternatives such as Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 122(A)(1)-(2)), all fixed with an ellipsis or the full limb.
4. (S09) When a battery hit list exceeds about 30 units, build the notes list from battery.py show with a script (unit id to citation), so every unit in the count is named (known shape 6) and versioned units keep their corpus spelling (e.g. 19-1505v1 as Okla. Stat. tit. 19, § 1505v1), which the citation resolver accepts.

### Proposed topic questions

- `deposit-cost-schedule` (S02): Does the deposit statute's list of what may be kept reach charges set by the lease itself (Oklahoma: 'other legally allowable charges under the provisions of this act or the rental agreement' (Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 115(B))), and if so does that phrase make a lease-set charge allowable or only refer to charges allowable on their own footing?
- `substandard-property-receivership` (S05): Does the state let cities foreclose code-violation liens in rem against non-owner-occupied (rental) property, with what threshold, waiting period and notice to occupants? (Oklahoma: Municipal Code Lien Enforcement Act of 2025, Okla. Stat. tit. 11, §§ 22-140 to 22-140.5, $1,500, six months, petition mailed to occupants.)
- `portfolio-thresholds` (S05): Do mechanic's-lien pre-lien notice or contractor-registration rules carve out small rental properties (e.g. four or fewer units, none owner-occupied), so a landlord can face a lien without warning? (Oklahoma: Okla. Stat. tit. 42, § 142.6(B)(3)(a); Okla. Stat. tit. 59, § 1151.9(B)(9).)
- `utility-allowance-cap` (S06): Does a separate statute cap what a landlord may charge for resold water and sewer service, apart from any electric and gas cap, and does it keep or drop the commercial-lease exception? (Oklahoma: Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1 for water and sewer, with no commercial-lease proviso; Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 161.1 for electricity and gas, with one.)
- `utility-transfer` (S06): Can a court in a protective-order case order a utility to transfer a household utility account to the protected person, so the landlord may see the account holder change mid-lease? (Oklahoma: Okla. Stat. tit. 22, § 60.4(J).)
- `utility-apportionment` (S06): Do the utility commission's rules let a landlord price natural gas into rent by formula while barring separate metering and billing of gas, and do they treat electricity differently? (Oklahoma: Okla. Admin. Code § 165:45-7-11(j) for gas; Okla. Admin. Code § 165:35-13-7(c), (e) for electricity.)
- `fire-sprinkler-duty` (S07): Does the state have a stand-alone high-rise sprinkler statute (Oklahoma: Okla. Stat. tit. 19, § 863.44D, every building over 75 feet built after May 27, 1975, with a daily penalty on the record owner) that applies whatever building code a locality adopts?
- `required-disclosures` (S09): Does a real estate commission rule require a landlord who holds a real estate license to disclose that status, or the license number, in the lease itself?
- `required-disclosures` (S09): Does the broker-relationships law require each party to confirm the broker's duty disclosure in a separate provision in or attached to the lease?
- `lease-term-limitation` (S09): Does a homestead-conveyance statute require both spouses to sign a lease of the homestead for longer than a set period, and does temporary renting keep the homestead character?
- `actual-notice-method` (S10): Does the state's own law (outside landlord-tenant) define actual notice, and does any residential rule (such as an eviction cure right) turn on actual notice to the landlord's rent agent rather than written notice?
- `guarantor-renewal` (S12): Does the state's general guaranty code release a guarantor when the creditor alters the principal obligation without the guarantor's consent, and must a guaranty signed after the lease have its own consideration? (Oklahoma: Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 338, Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 323.)
- `informal-dispute-resolution` (S12): Does the state's contract code void any stipulation restricting resort to the ordinary courts or limiting the time to sue (the Field Code rule: Okla. Stat. tit. 15, § 216, as in North Dakota and South Dakota), so that a mandatory pre-suit step must be drafted as a non-condition?

## Sync record (Claude Code, 2026-11-13)

- **Merge:** `merge-delta.py --base 8078d4e`: 249 new rows and 21 shared rows tagged OK (270, matching the running record and §2); library now 17,738 rows with the five §6 rows.

Stat. tit. 41, § 113(A)(3)), `tenant-maintenance-ok` (the tenant's duties in the words of Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 127), `early-termination-ok` (flat one-month fee; `rule_type` RECOMMENDED, not the proposed CONDITIONAL, after Taylor's 2026-11-12 decision) and `landlord-maintenance-ok` (cost on Tenant only for conditions caused deliberately or negligently, Okla. Stat. tit. 41, § 121(E)); OK untagged from `default-by-tenant-ks-ne`, `tenant-maintenance`, `early-termination-ks` and `landlord-maintenance`, each with a pointer segment. Row fixes: `tenant-caused-damage-ok` ('or household' dropped so its fault persons match `casualty-termination-ok`), `edu-tenant-repair-remedies-ok` and `edu-tenant-caused-damage-ok` (fault limb; actual notice to the rent agent, cash or cashier's check), `edu-foreign-ownership-ok` (the CFIUS and interstate-commerce proviso of Okla. Stat. tit. 60, § 121(B); stale currency note), `edu-fair-housing-ok` (owner-occupied exemption under the § 1453(C)(1) lead-in; surety bond only where restoration is needed), `edu-no-nuisance-eviction-rule-ok` (gambling leases void, Okla. Stat. tit. 21, §§ 945, 958; successive-owner liability, Okla. Stat. tit. 50, § 5), `edu-consumer-protection-ok` (notes: § 754 renumbered by Laws 2026, c. 387), `tenant-repair-agreement-ok` (citation moved out of the bracket; no consideration recital, since the agreement is made with the lease and § 118(B) asks only for a conspicuous independent writing), `edu-legal-interest-ok` (body rewritten: no statutory maximum for lease debts, the constitutional 10% usury rule, cautious course), `edu-statute-of-frauds-ok` (short leases protected only once the tenant is in possession), `edu-servicemember-rights-ok` (50 U.S.C. § 3951 eviction stay), `edu-utility-resale-ok` (water and sewer cap, Okla. Stat. tit. 17, § 151.1), `edu-holdover-ok` (Okla. Stat. tit. 23, § 70) and `pet-policy-ok` (fault limb on damage and odor only). Not applied: the optional `no-alterations` wording for the federal antenna rule (finding 24), since the clause's last sentence already defers to applicable law and a shared-text change would need every tagged state's vetting for no change in effect.

## NEEDS_REVIEW close-out (Claude Code, 2026-11-14)

- `edu-entity-landlord-court-representation-ok` is now VERIFIED. Taylor cleared OSCN's "verify you are human" check himself in his Chrome. Claude Code then read, through Claude in Chrome, the Rules Creating and Controlling the Oklahoma Bar Association, art. II, § 7(a): only active members of the Association may practice law, and that covers 'any other entity'. It also read art. II, § 6: in small claims an entity may proceed 'by and through a corporate officer or regular full-time employee'. That list is narrower than the 2024 statute's (Okla. Stat. tit. 12, § 1751(G)), so the cautious course now prefers an officer or a full-time employee. Claude Code also read the titles of all 41 Rules for District Courts, and Rule 31; none says who may appear for a party. Whether a non-lawyer appearing for an entity outside small claims is unauthorized practice is a question only case law could settle, so under the 2026-10-15 rule the row states the doubt and the cautious course (a licensed attorney).
- `edu-communal-land-lease-approval-ok` stays NEEDS_REVIEW, because the uncodified 1908 and 1947 acts can't be reached as text.
